<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every finite loopless <i>k</i>-edge-connected multigraph on at least two vertices, with <i>k</i> ≥ 1, has a spanning tree meeting each cut in at most $`C/k`$ times the size of the cut, where <i>C</i> is a universal constant. This resolves the strong thin tree conjecture.

- **claim-universal-thin-tree** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a universal constant $C>0$ such that, for every integer $k\ge1$, every finite $k$-edge-connected undirected multigraph without loops and with at least two vertices has a spanning tree $T$ satisfying $$\|\delta_T(S)\|\le\frac Ck\,\|\delta_G(S)\| \qquad\text{for every }\varnothing\ne S\subsetneq V.$$
  - Explanation: Every graph in the stated connectivity regime admits a spanning tree controlling all cuts simultaneously, with a universal inverse-connectivity factor.
- **claim-resistance-order** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-resistance-order)): For $X\succ0$, a column $b$, and $t>0$, $$b^{\mathsf T}X^{-1}b\le t \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad bb^{\mathsf T}\preceq tX.$$ If $X\succeq Y\succ0$, then $b^{\mathsf T}X^{-1}b\le b^{\mathsf T}Y^{-1}b$.
  - Explanation: A resistance bound is equivalent to a rank-one semidefinite bound, and increasing the positive definite matrix decreases resistance.
- **claim-incidence-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-incidence-rank)): If $F$ has $n$ vertices and $c$ connected components, its incidence columns span a space of dimension $n-c$. In particular the columns of a spanning tree form a basis of $\mathbf1^\perp$.
  - Explanation: Connected components determine incidence rank; spanning-tree incidence columns therefore give a basis of the zero-sum space.
- **claim-partition-packing-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-packing)): For a positive integer $r$, a graph $H$ contains $r$ edge-disjoint spanning trees if and only if $$\|E_H(\mathcal P)\|\ge r(\|\mathcal P\|-1) \qquad\text{for every partition }\mathcal P\text{ of }V(H).$$
  - Explanation: Packing edge-disjoint spanning trees is equivalent to meeting the crossing-edge lower bound for every vertex partition.
- **claim-maximal-packing-parts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-packing-parts)): Fix a positive integer $q$ and a graph $F$.* 1. *If $F[A]$ and $F[B]$ are $q$-tree-packable and $A\cap B\ne\varnothing$, then $F[A\cup B]$ is $q$-tree-packable.* 2. *The inclusion-maximal $q$-tree-packable vertex sets form a partition $\mathcal P(F)$. If $F\subseteq F'$, then $\mathcal P(F')$ coarsens $\mathcal P(F)$.* 3. *If $\|\mathcal P(F)\|>1$, then $$\|E_F(\mathcal P(F))\|<q(\|\mathcal P(F)\|-1).$$
  - Explanation: Overlapping packable sets merge. Their maximal members form nested partitions under edge addition, and a nontrivial maximal packing partition has too few crossing edges to support the prescribed quotient packing.
- **claim-polynomial-hierarchy-shortcut** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-hierarchy-power)): There is an absolute integer $q_H\ge2$ such that the following holds for each integer $q\ge q_H$. Let $H$ have a locally $q$-connected hierarchy, and let $\mathcal M$ be any collection of its nonroot nodes satisfying $\|O(A)\|\ge \|P(A)\|/q$. Then $H$ has a shortcut matrix $D$ such that $$\label{eq:hierarchy-power} \frac{1}{\|O(A)\|}\sum_{e\in O(A)}R_D(e)\le q^{-31/40} \qquad(A\in\mathcal M).$$ The cutoff is independent of the graph, the hierarchy depth and the marked collection $\mathcal M$.
  - Explanation: A locally connected hierarchy admits a shortcut controlling average resistance on every marked parent boundary, with an absolute cutoff independent of hierarchy depth.
- **claim-low-resistance-packing-extraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-extraction)): For all sufficiently large integers $r$, every $r$-tree-packable graph $H$ admits $X\succ0$ such that $$\label{eq:shortcut-X} L_H\preceq X,\qquad \mathbf1_S^{\mathsf T}X\mathbf1_S\le3\|\delta_H(S)\| \quad(\varnothing\ne S\subsetneq V),$$ and the edges with $R_X(e)\le4r^{-1/2}$ contain $q$ edge-disjoint spanning trees. The lower threshold on $r$ is independent of $H$ and $n$.
  - Explanation: The shortcut matrix dominates the Laplacian, has bounded cut quadratic forms, and leaves a large packing supported on low-resistance edges.
- **claim-simultaneous-matrix-response** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-response)): There is an absolute constant $C_0$, which may be taken to be $40$, such that for every $X\in\mathcal K$ there exists $Y\in\mathcal K$ with $$\label{eq:simultaneous-response} \bigl\|\{e\in B_s(X):R_Y(e)>r^{-1/2}\}\bigr\| \le C_0r^{-1/4}M_s(X) \qquad\text{for every }s>0.$$
  - Explanation: A single response matrix makes almost all crossing edges low resistance at every threshold partition simultaneously.
- **claim-rank-preserving-isotropic-representation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-auxiliary-representation)): Let $B$ be the union of $q\geq1$ edge-disjoint spanning trees on an $n$-vertex set, where $n\geq2$. For every $\rho>0$ there are vectors $z_e\in\mathbb R^{n-1}$, indexed by the individual edges of $B$, such that $$\sum_{e\in B}z_ez_e^{\mathsf T}=I_{n-1}, \qquad \\|z_e\\|^2\leq\frac{1+\rho}{q}. \label{eq:auxiliary-isotropy}$$ Moreover, there are an invertible linear map $M:\mathbf1^\perp\to \mathbb R^{n-1}$ and positive numbers $s_e$ such that $z_e=s_eMb_e$. Consequently the incidence vectors and the $z_e$ have the same rank on every subset of edges.
  - Explanation: Positive edge scalings and an invertible common map normalize the packed-tree incidence system while preserving the rank of every edge subset.
- **claim-projected-trace-certifies-packing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-projected-trace-packing)): With the vectors in Lemma <a href="#lem:auxiliary-representation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:auxiliary-representation">11</a>, suppose $A\subseteq E(B)$ and $c>0$ satisfy $$\sum_{e\in A}z_ez_e^{\mathsf T}\succeq cI_{n-1}.$$ Then $(V,A)$ contains at least $\lfloor cq/(1+\rho)\rfloor$ edge-disjoint spanning trees.
  - Explanation: A spectral lower bound in the rank-preserving representation implies every required partition inequality and hence a packing lower bound.
- **claim-two-sided-vector-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-two-system-split)): Let $w_e\in\mathbb R^D$ be finitely many vectors, let $\Sigma=\sum_e w_ew_e^{\mathsf T}\preceq2I_D$, and suppose that $\\|w_e\\|^2\leq\epsilon$ for every $e$, where $\epsilon>0$. There is a subset $A$ of the indices for which $$\frac12\Sigma-\tau I_D \preceq\sum_{e\in A}w_ew_e^{\mathsf T} \preceq\frac12\Sigma+\tau I_D, \qquad \tau=2\sqrt\epsilon+\epsilon. \label{eq:two-sided-split}$$
  - Explanation: The same subset approximates half the covariance from above and below, including when the covariance is singular.
- **claim-packing-preserving-sparsification** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-step)): There is an absolute integer $r_{\mathrm{step}}$ such that the following holds for every integer $r\geq r_{\mathrm{step}}$. If $H$ has at least two vertices and contains $r$ edge-disjoint spanning trees, set $$u=r^{-1/16},\qquad q=\lfloor(1-u)r\rfloor, \qquad \epsilon=6r^{-1/2},\qquad \tau=2\sqrt\epsilon+\epsilon.$$ Then $H$ has a spanning subgraph $H'$ containing at least $$r'=\left\lfloor\frac{(1-4u)r}{2}\right\rfloor \label{eq:step-packing-count}$$ edge-disjoint spanning trees, such that every nonempty proper $S\subset V(H)$ satisfies $$\|\delta_{H'}(S)\|\leq \left(\frac12+3\tau\right)\|\delta_H(S)\|. \label{eq:step-cut-bound}$$ The cutoff may be chosen so that, in addition, $$\frac5{16}\leq\frac{r'}r\leq\frac12, \qquad \frac{\frac12+3\tau}{r'/r}\leq1+15u. \label{eq:step-ratio-bound}$$
  - Explanation: A spanning subgraph retains about half the packed trees while reducing every cut by a comparable factor. The ratio estimate controls cumulative loss under iteration.
- **claim-isotropic-independent-vector-outcome** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-mss)): Let $\epsilon>0$, and let $v_1,\ldots,v_m$ be independent random vectors with finite support in $\mathbb C^d$. If $$\sum_{i=1}^m\mathbb E[v_iv_i^*]=I_d, \qquad \mathbb E\\|v_i\\|^2\leq\epsilon\quad(1\leq i\leq m), \label{eq:mss-hypotheses}$$ then $$\mathbb P\left\{ \left\\|\sum_{i=1}^m v_iv_i^*\right\\| \leq(1+\sqrt\epsilon)^2\right\}>0. \label{eq:mss-conclusion}$$ Here $*$ denotes conjugate transpose and the norm is the operator norm. In particular, some outcome satisfies the corresponding positive-semidefinite upper bound. Real vectors are a special case.
  - Explanation: Independent finitely supported vectors with isotropic total covariance and bounded expected squared norms have a positive-probability outcome with the asserted operator-norm bound.
- **claim-polynomial-limit-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mss-polynomial-limits)): Suppose nonzero univariate polynomials $f_\nu$ have uniformly bounded degrees and converge coefficientwise to a nonzero polynomial $f$. If all $f_\nu$ have no zeros in $\mathbb H$, neither does $f$. If all $f_\nu$ are real-rooted, so is $f$. For monic real-rooted polynomials of a fixed degree, their ordered roots depend continuously on their coefficients.
  - Explanation: Bounded-degree coefficient limits preserve upper-half-plane zero exclusion and real-rootedness. Ordered roots are continuous for monic real-rooted polynomials of fixed degree.
- **claim-elementary-stability-operations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mss-stability)): The following assertions hold.* 1. *If $A_1,\ldots,A_m$ are Hermitian positive-semidefinite matrices and $C$ is Hermitian, then $$\det\left(xI+C+\sum_{i=1}^m z_iA_i\right)$$ is real stable in $x,z_1,\ldots,z_m$. If $\sum_iA_i$ is positive definite, then $\det(\sum_i z_iA_i)$ is also real stable.* 2. *If $p$ is real stable, then $(1-\partial_i)p$ is real stable.* 3. *A nonzero univariate polynomial obtained by setting all but one variable of a real stable polynomial to real constants is real-rooted.
  - Explanation: Positive-semidefinite determinant pencils are real stable, the indicated differential operator preserves stability, and nonzero real univariate specializations are real-rooted.
- **claim-mixed-polynomial-expectation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mss-expectation)): The polynomial in <a href="#eq:mss-mixed-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mss-mixed-polynomial">[eq:mss-mixed-polynomial]</a> is monic of degree $d$ and real-rooted. If $v_1,\ldots,v_m$ are independent finitely supported random vectors and $A_i=\mathbb E[v_iv_i^*]$, then $$\mathbb E\det\left(xI_d-\sum_{i=1}^m v_iv_i^*\right) =\mu[A_1,\ldots,A_m](x). \label{eq:mss-expectation-identity}$$
  - Explanation: The mixed characteristic polynomial is monic and real-rooted, and independence identifies it with the expected characteristic polynomial of the random outer-product sum.
- **claim-common-interlacing-selection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mss-common-interlacing)): If every convex combination of finitely many monic degree-$d$ polynomials is real-rooted, they have a common interlacing. Consequently, for any probability weights on this collection, some polynomial of positive weight has largest root at most the largest root of the weighted average.
  - Explanation: Real-rootedness of every convex combination forces common interlacing, which permits selection of a positive-weight polynomial without increasing the largest root.
- **claim-selection-from-independent-supports** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mss-selection)): For independent finitely supported vectors $v_1,\ldots,v_m$, some outcome of positive probability has largest eigenvalue of $\sum_i v_iv_i^*$ at most the largest root of $\mu[\mathbb E v_1v_1^*,\ldots,\mathbb E v_mv_m^*]$.
  - Explanation: Repeated conditional interlacing selects a complete positive-probability outcome whose largest eigenvalue is bounded by the largest root of the initial mixed polynomial.
- **claim-positive-barrier-partial-fractions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mss-partial-fractions)): Let $p$ be real stable, $z\in\operatorname{Ab}_p$, and let $i,j$ be coordinate indices. As a rational function of $t$, $$\Phi_p^i(z+te_j) =c+\sum_{\ell=1}^s\frac{c_\ell}{t-\lambda_\ell}, \qquad c\geq0,\quad c_\ell>0,\quad\lambda_\ell<0, \label{eq:mss-positive-partial-fractions}$$ where the sum may be empty. In particular, for $t\geq0$ this function is nonnegative, nonincreasing, and convex.
  - Explanation: Above the roots, each coordinate barrier along a coordinate ray has a positive partial-fraction expansion, yielding nonnegativity, monotonicity, and convexity.
- **claim-barrier-shift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mss-barrier-shift)): Let $p$ be real stable, let $z\in\operatorname{Ab}_p$, and suppose $\delta>0$ satisfies $$\Phi_p^j(z)+\frac1\delta\leq1. \label{eq:mss-shift-hypothesis}$$ For $q=(1-\partial_j)p$ and $w=z+\delta e_j$, one has $w\in\operatorname{Ab}_q$ and $$\Phi_q^i(w)\leq\Phi_p^i(z)\qquad\text{for every }i. \label{eq:mss-shift-conclusion}$$
  - Explanation: The barrier hypothesis permits applying the differential operator and shifting the selected coordinate while preserving the above-roots region and controlling every barrier.
- **claim-mixed-polynomial-largest-root** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mss-root-bound)): If $A_i\succeq0$, $\sum_iA_i=I_d$, and $\operatorname{tr}A_i\leq\epsilon$ for every $i$, where $\epsilon>0$, then the largest root of $\mu[A_1,\ldots,A_m]$ is at most $(1+\sqrt\epsilon)^2$.
  - Explanation: Isotropic covariance and the individual trace bound control the largest root through successive barrier shifts.
- **claim-shortcut-dual-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hierarchy-dual)): With the preceding notation, set $$\label{hd:ratio} Q_*= \sup_{\substack{h\geq m,\ X\in\{0,1\}^{h\times V},\ \mathcal E(X)>0\\ U\in\mathbb R^{E\times h},\ UU^{\mathsf T}=I_m}} \frac{\displaystyle \sum_{t\in\mathcal M}\frac1{\|O(t)\|} \left(\sum_{e\in O(t)}\langle u_e,Xb_e\rangle\right)^2} {\mathcal E(X)}.$$ Then $$\label{hd:comparison} p_*\leq Q_*\leq2p_*.$$ Consequently, if every ratio in <a href="#hd:ratio" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="hd:ratio">[hd:ratio]</a> is at most $K$, then for every $\eta>0$ there is a full positive definite matrix $D$ satisfying all the cut constraints in <a href="#hd:primal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="hd:primal">[hd:primal]</a> and all the marked-node average-resistance bounds with right side $K+\eta$.
  - Explanation: The shortcut infimum is comparable to a supremum over binary embeddings and orthonormal edge vectors. Strict slack yields a feasible full positive definite matrix without assuming primal attainment.
- **claim-spectral-packing-of-endpoint-images** (construction, [source](#stmt-hb-spectral-packing)): Let $F$ be a nonempty set of oriented edges of a finite multigraph with edge set $E$, and put $m=\|F\|$. For points $Y_v\in\mathbb R^E$, write $Y_e=Y_u-Y_v$ when $e=(u,v)$, and denote the $e$-coordinate of $Y_e$ by $Y_{e,e}$. Suppose that $a>0$ and $$\label{hb:spectral-assumption} \Upsilon:=\left(\frac1m\sum_{e\in F}Y_{e,e}\right)^2>0, \qquad \Upsilon\ge\frac a m\sum_{e\in F}\\|Y_e\\|_2^2.$$ Then $a\le1$, and there are $b\ge2$ pairwise disjoint Euclidean balls of one positive squared radius $r$, centered at images of endpoints of edges in $F$, such that $$\label{hb:spectral-logarithmic} b\ge\frac{am}{16}, \qquad br\ge\frac{9m\Upsilon} {512\bigl(5+\log_2(1/a)\bigr)^2}.$$ Consequently, for every $0<\varepsilon<1/3$, the choice $$\label{hb:spectral-constant} C_1(\varepsilon)=\frac{1024}{\varepsilon^2}$$ gives $$\label{hb:spectral-conclusion} b\ge\frac{a\|F\|}{C_1(\varepsilon)}, \qquad br\ge\frac{a^\varepsilon\Upsilon\|F\|}{C_1(\varepsilon)}.$$
  - Explanation: A positive diagonal-coordinate signal relative to edge energy forces many separated endpoint images. A singular-value tail gives a radius and a lower bound on total squared-radius mass.
- **claim-homogeneous-dominating-truncation** (construction, [source](#stmt-hb-bucketing)): Define $$J=1+\left\lceil\log_2(256/\alpha^2)\right\rceil, \qquad \gamma=\frac1{4J^4}.$$ Every $\alpha$-bad node with a positive boundary sum admits a $2$-homogeneous, $\gamma$-dominating subset $O'(t)$.
  - Explanation: Truncation followed by dyadic bucketing retains a dominating boundary subset with controlled variation in both edge quantities.
- **claim-homogeneous-bag-family** (construction, [source](#stmt-hb-homogeneous-bags)): Let $T$ be a nonempty set of $\alpha$-bad nodes with positive boundary sums, and let $0<\gamma\le1$. Suppose that $O'(t)$ is $\gamma$-dominating for each $t\in T$ and that $F=\bigcup_{t\in T}O'(t)$ is $4$-homogeneous. Write $$s=\frac{\alpha\gamma}{32},\qquad c_1=\min_{e\in F}a_e^2, \qquad S'=\sum_{t\in T}\frac1{\|O(t)\|} \left(\sum_{e\in O'(t)}a_e\right)^2.$$ For $\beta>1$ and $0<\epsilon<1/3$, put $C=C_1(\epsilon)=1024\epsilon^{-2}$. If $$\label{hb:smallness} s^\epsilon\le\frac1{48\beta C},$$ there is either a family of $\beta$-compact bags or a family of $s^{1+2\epsilon}$-assigned bags, assigned to a subset of $T$, whose mass is at least $$\label{hb:one-mass} \frac{s^\epsilon}{192\beta C}\,S'.$$ Its radius, and also its diameter parameter in the compact case, belong to $[sc_1,12c_1/s]$.
  - Explanation: Homogeneous dominating boundary subsets produce either compact bags or assigned bags with controlled mass and radius range.
- **claim-geometric-bag-extraction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-hierarchy-bags)): Let $T$ be a nonempty finite set of $\alpha$-bad nodes with positive boundary sums, where $0<\alpha\le1$. Define $$\begin{aligned} J&=1+\lceil\log_2(256/\alpha^2)\rceil, &C_2(\alpha)&=128J^4, &s&=\alpha/C_2(\alpha),\\ L&=2+\lceil\log_2(1/s)\rceil, &H&=1+\lceil\log_2(24/(\lambda s^2))\rceil, &C_1(\epsilon)&=1024\epsilon^{-2}. \end{aligned}$$ Suppose $\beta>1$, $0<\epsilon<1/3$, $0<\lambda<1$, and $s^\epsilon\le(48\beta C_1(\epsilon))^{-1}$. There is a $\lambda$-geometric sequence of families, consisting either entirely of $\beta$-compact bags or entirely of $s^{1+2\epsilon}$-assigned bags assigned to distinct nodes of $T$, whose mass satisfies $$\label{hb:extraction-bound} \mathsf M\ge \frac{s^\epsilon}{384\beta C_1(\epsilon)C_2(\alpha)LH} \sum_{t\in T}\frac1{\|O(t)\|} \left(\sum_{e\in O(t)}a_e\right)^2.$$ In particular, all losses are functions of $\alpha,\epsilon,\beta$ and $\lambda$, independent of the number of vertices and hierarchy depth.
  - Explanation: Grouping homogeneous boundary data by scale and retaining separated scales produces a geometric sequence whose losses depend on the parameters rather than graph size or hierarchy depth.
- **claim-compact-bag-charging** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-hierarchy-compact)): Let $G=(V,E)$ be a finite $k$-edge-connected multigraph, and let $X_v\in Q=[0,1]^h$ for $v\in V$. Suppose $(\mathcal F_i)$ is a finite or countable $\lambda$-geometric sequence of nonempty families of $\beta$-compact bags, of respective types $(\delta_i,\Delta_i)$, where $0<\lambda\le 1/12$ and $\beta\ge36$. Thus all balls in $\mathcal F_i$ are mutually disjoint ordinary balls $B_Q(X_v,\delta_i)$, every bag $D\in\mathcal F_i$ has at least two balls and center diameter at most $\Delta_i$, and $$\beta\Delta_i\le \|D\|\delta_i, \qquad \Delta_{i+1}\le\lambda\delta_i.$$ Then $$\label{hc:conclusion} \frac{k}{4}\sum_i\delta_i\sum_{D\in\mathcal F_i}\|D\| \le \sum_{e\in E}\\|X_e\\|_1, \qquad X_{\{u,v\}}=X_u-X_v.$$
  - Explanation: Total compact-bag mass is bounded by graph edge length after scaling by connectivity, including countable geometric sequences.
- **claim-disjoint-shell-length-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-hc-length)): Let $G=(V,E)$ be a finite $k$-edge-connected multigraph, where $k>0$, and let $X_v\in Q=[0,1]^h$ for $v\in V$. Let $\mathcal Z$ be a finite collection of pairwise disjoint ordinary or hollowed balls in $Q$. For each $H\in\mathcal Z$, write $x_H,a_H,b_H$ for its center and numerical inner and outer radii. Suppose there are vertices $u_H,v_H\in V$ such that $$\label{hc:witness} \\|X_{u_H}-x_H\\|_1\le a_H, \qquad \\|X_{v_H}-x_H\\|_1\ge b_H.$$ Then $$\label{hc:length-bound} k\sum_{H\in\mathcal Z}(b_H-a_H) \le \sum_{\{u,v\}\in E}\\|X_u-X_v\\|_1.$$
  - Explanation: Connectivity across radial level cuts and disjointness of charged geometric portions bound the sum of shell widths by graph length.
- **claim-assigned-bag-charging** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-hierarchy-assigned)): Let $X:V\to\{0,1\}^{d}$, and let $\mathcal T$ be a locally $k$-connected hierarchy of $G$, with selected nonroot nodes $T$ satisfying $\|O(t)\|\ge k\lambda\|P(t)\|$ for $t\in T$. Suppose $\mathcal F_1,\ldots,\mathcal F_m$ is a $\lambda$-geometric sequence of families of $(24C_3/k)$-assigned bags, of types $(\delta_i,T_i)$, with $T_i\subseteq T$. If $$C_4\ge3,\qquad \lambda\le\frac1{6C_4},\qquad C_3\ge2\bigl((C_4+1)+4(C_4+2)^2\bigr),$$ then $$\label{ha:assigned-bound} \sum_{\{u,v\}\in E}\\|X_u-X_v\\|_1 \ \ge\ \frac{k}{8}\frac{C_4}{12C_3} \sum_{i=1}^m\delta_i\sum_{t\in T_i}\|\operatorname{Bag}_t\|.$$ In particular, one may take $C_4=3$, $C_3=10^4$ and $\lambda\le1/18$.
  - Explanation: Local connectivity and the marked boundary ratio allow assigned-bag mass to be charged to graph length through valid labeled shells.
- **claim-edge-disjoint-paths** (construction, [source](#stmt-ha-edge-paths)): If every cut separating distinct vertices $u,v$ of an undirected multigraph has at least $q$ edges, where $q$ is a positive integer, then the graph has $q$ pairwise edge-disjoint $u$–$v$ paths.
  - Explanation: Integral augmentations produce edge-disjoint paths whenever every separating cut meets the prescribed edge lower bound.
- **claim-predecessor-pruning-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-ha-pruning)): Suppose $\delta_{i+1}\le\lambda\delta_i$ with $\lambda\le1/2$, $\|O(t)\|\ge k\lambda\|P(t)\|$ for the selected hierarchy nodes, and each bag is at least $C_3/k$-assigned. For $s\in T_\ell$, $$\sum_i\delta_i \|\{B\in\mathcal B_i:B\text{ is noninsertable by }s\}\| \le \frac{4}{C_3}\delta_\ell\|\operatorname{Bag}_s\|.$$
  - Explanation: Disjointness within each scale and geometric decay bound the total mass made noninsertable by an earlier selected node.
- **claim-valid-labeling-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-ha-labeled-energy)): Every finite collection $Z$ with a valid labeling satisfies $$\frac{k}{4}\sum_{B\in Z}\operatorname{width}(B) \le \sum_{\{v,w\}\in E}\\|X_v-X_w\\|_1.$$
  - Explanation: Witness paths charge shell widths to distinct portions of graph edges. Intersecting geometric records cannot double-charge a common routing domain.
- **claim-insertable-bag-arrangement** (construction, [source](#stmt-ha-insertable-arrangement)): Let $\mathcal T$ be a locally $k$-connected hierarchy, with selected nonroot nodes $T$ satisfying $\|O(t)\|\ge k\lambda\|P(t)\|$ for $t\in T$. Let $\mathcal F_i$ be a $\lambda$-geometric sequence of families of $12C_3/k$-assigned bags of type $(\delta_i,T_i)$, where the $T_i\subseteq T$ are pairwise disjoint. Suppose all input balls are insertable and $$C_4\ge3,\qquad \lambda\le\frac{1}{6C_4},\qquad C_3\ge2\bigl((C_4+1)+4(C_4+2)^2\bigr).$$ Then there is a finite valid collection $Z$ of labeled ordinary and hollow balls, with centers inherited from the input balls, such that $$\label{ha:arrangement-width} \sum_{B\in Z}\operatorname{width}(B) \ge \frac{C_4}{12C_3} \sum_i\delta_i\sum_{t\in T_i}\|\operatorname{Bag}_t\|.$$
  - Explanation: Insertable assigned bags admit a finite valid labeled arrangement whose total width captures a fixed fraction of their mass.
- **claim-insertable-input-validity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ha-input-valid)): An insertable ball $B=B(X_u,\delta_i)\in\operatorname{Bag}_t$ with the input labels above satisfies <a href="#ha:L1" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ha:L1">[ha:L1]</a>–<a href="#ha:L3" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ha:L3">[ha:L3]</a>.
  - Explanation: Insertability supplies the excluded-boundary condition, while local connectivity supplies the crossing paths needed for individual record validity.
- **claim-conflict-domain-ancestor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ha-ancestor)): While $\operatorname{Bag}_t\in\mathcal F_\ell$ is being processed, if a candidate $A\in\operatorname{Bag}_t$ and a current record $B$ have intersecting conflict domains, then $t(B)$ is a weak ancestor of $t$, and $t\in C(B)$.
  - Explanation: The depth order and predecessor exclusions force an existing record with an intersecting conflict domain to be owned by an ancestor of the node being processed.
- **claim-unique-interior-record** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ha-unique-interior)): Under the preceding hypotheses, suppose $C(A)\cap C(B)\ne\varnothing$. If another current record $D\ne B$ intersects $B$, then $C(A)\cap C(D)=\varnothing$. Consequently a candidate is interior to at most one current record.
  - Explanation: Pairwise record validity and the ancestor relation make an interior record unique for each candidate.
- **claim-separation-heredity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ha-heredity)): Suppose $B,D$ satisfy condition <a href="#ha:L4" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ha:L4">35</a>. If a new record $B'$ has support contained in $B$ and $C(B')\subseteq C(B)$, then $B',D$ also satisfy that condition. A concentric radial restriction of $B$ retaining all its labels also retains conditions <a href="#ha:L1" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ha:L1">[ha:L1]</a>–<a href="#ha:L3" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ha:L3">[ha:L3]</a>, if it has positive width.
  - Explanation: Restricting both support and conflict domain preserves separation from other records; positive-width concentric restrictions retaining labels preserve individual validity.
- **claim-border-classification-monotonicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ha-border-monotone)): During the processing of this phase, a candidate which is a border ball never becomes interior. In particular every border ball retained for the end of a bag’s processing remains a border ball at the end of the phase.
  - Explanation: Inserted original balls cannot create an interior with the required margins, and concentric restrictions cannot create a new interior condition.
- **claim-interior-processing-potential** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ha-construction)): Suppose phase $\ell$ starts with a valid arrangement whose potentials can pay each earlier node $v\in T_i$, $i<\ell$, at least $(\theta/2)\|\operatorname{Bag}_v\|\delta_i$. Interior processing preserves validity and these allocations. At every intermediate state it also pays each completed node $v\in T_\ell$ at least $\theta n_{v,\infty}\delta_\ell$, and the node currently being processed at least $\theta n_{t,s}\delta_\ell$. Each bag finishes with $\|\operatorname{Int}_{t,\infty}\|<\|\operatorname{Bag}_t\|/2$.
  - Explanation: Each carving operation preserves validity and existing allocations while earning credit for treated balls; the interior loop terminates below the half-bag threshold.
- **claim-phase-shrink-deficit** (bound, [source](#stmt-ha-shrink-cost)): Under <a href="#ha:small-excess" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ha:small-excess">[ha:small-excess]</a>, the total token deficit created by the restrictions satisfies $$\mathsf D:=\sum_{B\in Z}\bigl(p(B)-q(S(B))\bigr) \le\frac12 b\delta_\ell.$$ Every summand defining $\mathsf D$ is nonnegative.
  - Explanation: When the phase-transition excess is small, restricting old records creates a nonnegative total deficit that border-ball credit can cover.
- **claim-compact-convex-fixed-point** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-fixedpoint)): Let $K$ be a nonempty compact convex subset of a finite-dimensional real vector space. Every continuous map $F:K\to K$ has a fixed point.
  - Explanation: Every continuous self-map of the stated compact convex domain has a fixed point.
- **claim-sperner-parity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lemma-sperner)): Label every vertex $v$ of a finite triangulation of $\Delta_d$ by an integer $\ell(v)\in\{0,\ldots,d\}$, with $v_{\ell(v)}>0$. The number of $d$-simplices whose vertex labels are $0,\ldots,d$, each occurring once, is odd.
  - Explanation: A face-compatible labeling of a finite simplex triangulation has an odd number of fully labeled top-dimensional simplices.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lemma-two-system-split",
        "exact_quote": "Let $w_e\\in\\mathbb R^D$ be finitely many vectors, let $\\Sigma=\\sum_e w_ew_e^{\\mathsf T}\\preceq2I_D$, and suppose that $\\|w_e\\|^2\\leq\\epsilon$ for every $e$, where $\\epsilon>0$.         "source_id": "PM-174.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-174.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-step",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute integer $r_{\\mathrm{step}}$ such that the following holds for every integer $r\\geq r_{\\mathrm{step}}$. If $H$ has at least two vertices and contains $r$ edge-disjoint spanning trees, set $$u=r^{-1/16},\\qquad q=\\lfloor(1-u)r\\rfloor,\n \\qquad \\epsilon=6r^{-1/2},\\qquad\n \\tau=2\\sqrt\\epsilon+\\epsilon.$$ Then $H$ has a spanning subgraph $H'$ containing at least $$r'=\\left\\lfloor\\frac{(1-4u)r}{2}\\right\\rfloor\n \\label{eq:step-packing-count}$$ edge-disjoint spanning trees, such that every nonempty proper $S\\subset V(H)$ satisfies $$|\\delta_{H'}(S)|\\leq\n \\left(\\frac12+3\\tau\\right)|\\delta_H(S)|.\n \\label{eq:step-cut-bound}$$ The cutoff may be chosen so that, in addition, $$\\frac5{16}\\leq\\frac{r'}r\\leq\\frac12,\n \\qquad\n \\frac{\\frac12+3\\tau}{r'/r}\\leq1+15u.\n \\label{eq:step-ratio-bound}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-174.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-step",
          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute integer $r_{\\mathrm{step}}$ such that the following holds for every integer $r\\geq r_{\\mathrm{step}}$.           "source_id": "PM-174.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-mss-polynomial-limits",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose nonzero univariate polynomials $f_\\nu$ have uniformly bounded degrees and converge coefficientwise to a nonzero polynomial $f$. If all $f_\\nu$ have no zeros in $\\mathbb H$, neither does $f$. If all $f_\\nu$ are real-rooted, so is $f$. For monic real-rooted polynomials of a fixed degree, their ordered roots depend continuously on their coefficients.",
        "source_id": "PM-174.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-mss-stability",
        "exact_quote": "The following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *If $A_1,\\ldots,A_m$ are Hermitian positive-semidefinite matrices and $C$ is Hermitian, then $$\\det\\left(xI+C+\\sum_{i=1}^m z_iA_i\\right)$$ is real stable in $x,z_1,\\ldots,z_m$. If $\\sum_iA_i$ is positive definite, then $\\det(\\sum_i z_iA_i)$ is also real stable.*\n\n2.  *If $p$ is real stable, then $(1-\\partial_i)p$ is real stable.*\n\n3.  *A nonzero univariate polynomial obtained by setting all but one variable of a real stable polynomial to real constants is real-rooted.",
        "source_id": "PM-174.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-mss-stability",
          "exact_quote": "The following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *If $A_1,\\ldots,A_m$ are Hermitian positive-semidefinite matrices and $C$ is Hermitian, then $$\\det\\left(xI+C+\\sum_{i=1}^m z_iA_i\\right)$$ is real stable in $x,z_1,\\ldots,z_m$.         "exact_quote": "With the preceding notation, set $$\\label{hd:ratio}\n Q_*=\n \\sup_{\\substack{h\\geq m,\\ X\\in\\{0,1\\}^{h\\times V},\\ \\mathcal E(X)>0\\\\\n                  U\\in\\mathbb R^{E\\times h},\\ UU^{\\mathsf T}=I_m}}\n \\frac{\\displaystyle\n       \\sum_{t\\in\\mathcal M}\\frac1{|O(t)|}\n       \\left(\\sum_{e\\in O(t)}\\langle u_e,Xb_e\\rangle\\right)^2}\n      {\\mathcal E(X)}.$$ Then $$\\label{hd:comparison}\n p_*\\leq Q_*\\leq2p_*.$$ Consequently, if every ratio in <a href=\"#hd:ratio\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hd:ratio\">[hd:ratio]</a> is at most $K$, then for every $\\eta>0$ there is a full positive definite matrix $D$ satisfying all the cut constraints in <a href=\"#hd:primal\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hd:primal\">[hd:primal]</a> and all the marked-node average-resistance bounds with right side $K+\\eta$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hb-spectral-packing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a nonempty set of oriented edges of a finite multigraph with edge set $E$, and put $m=|F|$. For points $Y_v\\in\\mathbb R^E$, write $Y_e=Y_u-Y_v$ when $e=(u,v)$, and denote the $e$-coordinate of $Y_e$ by $Y_{e,e}$. Suppose that $a>0$ and $$\\label{hb:spectral-assumption}\n \\Upsilon:=\\left(\\frac1m\\sum_{e\\in F}Y_{e,e}\\right)^2>0,\n \\qquad\n \\Upsilon\\ge\\frac a m\\sum_{e\\in F}\\|Y_e\\|_2^2.$$ Then $a\\le1$, and there are $b\\ge2$ pairwise disjoint Euclidean balls of one positive squared radius $r$, centered at images of endpoints of edges in $F$, such that $$\\label{hb:spectral-logarithmic}\n b\\ge\\frac{am}{16},\n \\qquad\n br\\ge\\frac{9m\\Upsilon}\n {512\\bigl(5+\\log_2(1/a)\\bigr)^2}.$$ Consequently, for every $0<\\varepsilon<1/3$, the choice $$\\label{hb:spectral-constant}\n C_1(\\varepsilon)=\\frac{1024}{\\varepsilon^2}$$ gives $$\\label{hb:spectral-conclusion}\n b\\ge\\frac{a|F|}{C_1(\\varepsilon)},\n \\qquad\n br\\ge\\frac{a^\\varepsilon\\Upsilon|F|}{C_1(\\varepsilon)}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-174.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-hb-spectral-packing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a nonempty set of oriented edges of a finite multigraph with edge set $E$, and put $m=|F|$. Suppose that $a>0$ and $$\\label{hb:spectral-assumption}\n \\Upsilon:=\\left(\\frac1m\\sum_{e\\in F}Y_{e,e}\\right)^2>0,\n \\qquad\n \\Upsilon\\ge\\frac a m\\sum_{e\\in F}\\|Y_e\\|_2^2.$$ Then $a\\le1$, and there are $b\\ge2$ pairwise disjoint Euclidean balls of one positive squared radius $r$, centered at images of endpoints of edges in $F$, such that $$\\label{hb:spectral-logarithmic}\n b\\ge\\frac{am}{16},\n \\qquad\n br\\ge\\frac{9m\\Upsilon}\n {512\\bigl(5+\\log_2(1/a)\\bigr)^2}.$$ Consequently, for every $0<\\varepsilon<1/3$, the choice $$\\label{hb:spectral-constant}\n C_1(\\varepsilon)=\\frac{1024}{\\varepsilon^2}$$ gives $$\\label{hb:spectral-conclusion}\n b\\ge\\frac{a|F|}{C_1(\\varepsilon)},\n \\qquad\n br\\ge\\frac{a^\\varepsilon\\Upsilon|F|}{C_1(\\varepsilon)}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-174.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-hb-homogeneous-bags",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a nonempty set of $\\alpha$-bad nodes with positive boundary sums, and let $0<\\gamma\\le1$. Suppose that $O'(t)$ is $\\gamma$-dominating for each $t\\in T$ and that $F=\\bigcup_{t\\in T}O'(t)$ is $4$-homogeneous. Write $$s=\\frac{\\alpha\\gamma}{32},\\qquad\n c_1=\\min_{e\\in F}a_e^2,\n \\qquad\n S'=\\sum_{t\\in T}\\frac1{|O(t)|}\n                     \\left(\\sum_{e\\in O'(t)}a_e\\right)^2.$$ For $\\beta>1$ and $0<\\epsilon<1/3$, put $C=C_1(\\epsilon)=1024\\epsilon^{-2}$. If $$\\label{hb:smallness}\n s^\\epsilon\\le\\frac1{48\\beta C},$$ there is either a family of $\\beta$-compact bags or a family of $s^{1+2\\epsilon}$-assigned bags, assigned to a subset of $T$, whose mass is at least $$\\label{hb:one-mass}\n \\frac{s^\\epsilon}{192\\beta C}\\,S'.$$ Its radius, and also its diameter parameter in the compact case, belong to $[sc_1,12c_1/s]$.",
        "source_id": "PM-174.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-hb-homogeneous-bags",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a nonempty set of $\\alpha$-bad nodes with positive boundary sums, and let $0<\\gamma\\le1$. If $$\\label{hb:smallness}\n s^\\epsilon\\le\\frac1{48\\beta C},$$ there is either a family of $\\beta$-compact bags or a family of $s^{1+2\\epsilon}$-assigned bags, assigned to a subset of $T$, whose mass is at least $$\\label{hb:one-mass}\n \\frac{s^\\epsilon}{192\\beta C}\\,S'.$$ Its radius, and also its diameter parameter in the compact case, belong to $[sc_1,12c_1/s]$.",
          "source_id": "PM-174.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-hierarchy-bags",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a nonempty finite set of $\\alpha$-bad nodes with positive boundary sums, where $0<\\alpha\\le1$. Define $$\\begin{aligned}\n J&=1+\\lceil\\log_2(256/\\alpha^2)\\rceil,\n &C_2(\\alpha)&=128J^4,\n &s&=\\alpha/C_2(\\alpha),\\\\\n L&=2+\\lceil\\log_2(1/s)\\rceil,\n &H&=1+\\lceil\\log_2(24/(\\lambda s^2))\\rceil,\n &C_1(\\epsilon)&=1024\\epsilon^{-2}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Suppose $\\beta>1$, $0<\\epsilon<1/3$, $0<\\lambda<1$, and $s^\\epsilon\\le(48\\beta C_1(\\epsilon))^{-1}$. There is a $\\lambda$-geometric sequence of families, consisting either entirely of $\\beta$-compact bags or entirely of $s^{1+2\\epsilon}$-assigned bags assigned to distinct nodes of $T$, whose mass satisfies $$\\label{hb:extraction-bound}\n \\mathsf M\\ge\n \\frac{s^\\epsilon}{384\\beta C_1(\\epsilon)C_2(\\alpha)LH}\n \\sum_{t\\in T}\\frac1{|O(t)|}\n                  \\left(\\sum_{e\\in O(t)}a_e\\right)^2.$$ In particular, all losses are functions of $\\alpha,\\epsilon,\\beta$ and $\\lambda$, independent of the number of vertices and hierarchy depth.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-hierarchy-compact",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G=(V,E)$ be a finite $k$-edge-connected multigraph, and let $X_v\\in Q=[0,1]^h$ for $v\\in V$. Suppose $(\\mathcal F_i)$ is a finite or countable $\\lambda$-geometric sequence of nonempty families of $\\beta$-compact bags, of respective types $(\\delta_i,\\Delta_i)$, where $0<\\lambda\\le 1/12$ and $\\beta\\ge36$. Thus all balls in $\\mathcal F_i$ are mutually disjoint ordinary balls $B_Q(X_v,\\delta_i)$, every bag $D\\in\\mathcal F_i$ has at least two balls and center diameter at most $\\Delta_i$, and $$\\beta\\Delta_i\\le |D|\\delta_i,\n \\qquad\n \\Delta_{i+1}\\le\\lambda\\delta_i.$$ Then $$\\label{hc:conclusion}\n \\frac{k}{4}\\sum_i\\delta_i\\sum_{D\\in\\mathcal F_i}|D|\n \\le \\sum_{e\\in E}\\|X_e\\|_1,\n \\qquad X_{\\{u,v\\}}=X_u-X_v.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-174.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $G=(V,E)$ be a finite $k$-edge-connected multigraph, where $k>0$, and let $X_v\\in Q=[0,1]^h$ for $v\\in V$. Let $\\mathcal Z$ be a finite collection of pairwise disjoint ordinary or hollowed balls in $Q$. For each $H\\in\\mathcal Z$, write $x_H,a_H,b_H$ for its center and numerical inner and outer radii.         "source_id": "PM-174.1"

        "exact_quote": "Let $X:V\\to\\{0,1\\}^{d}$, and let $\\mathcal T$ be a locally $k$-connected hierarchy of $G$, with selected nonroot nodes $T$ satisfying $|O(t)|\\ge k\\lambda|P(t)|$ for $t\\in T$. If $$C_4\\ge3,\\qquad \\lambda\\le\\frac1{6C_4},\\qquad\n C_3\\ge2\\bigl((C_4+1)+4(C_4+2)^2\\bigr),$$ then $$\\label{ha:assigned-bound}\n \\sum_{\\{u,v\\}\\in E}\\|X_u-X_v\\|_1\n \\ \\ge\\ \\frac{k}{8}\\frac{C_4}{12C_3}\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\delta_i\\sum_{t\\in T_i}|\\operatorname{Bag}_t|.$$ In particular, one may take $C_4=3$, $C_3=10^4$ and $\\lambda\\le1/18$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-ha-insertable-arrangement",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal T$ be a locally $k$-connected hierarchy, with selected nonroot nodes $T$ satisfying $|O(t)|\\ge k\\lambda|P(t)|$ for $t\\in T$. Let $\\mathcal F_i$ be a $\\lambda$-geometric sequence of families of $12C_3/k$-assigned bags of type $(\\delta_i,T_i)$, where the $T_i\\subseteq T$ are pairwise disjoint. Suppose all input balls are insertable and $$C_4\\ge3,\\qquad \\lambda\\le\\frac{1}{6C_4},\\qquad\n C_3\\ge2\\bigl((C_4+1)+4(C_4+2)^2\\bigr).$$ Then there is a finite valid collection $Z$ of labeled ordinary and hollow balls, with centers inherited from the input balls, such that $$\\label{ha:arrangement-width}\n \\sum_{B\\in Z}\\operatorname{width}(B)\n \\ge \\frac{C_4}{12C_3}\n       \\sum_i\\delta_i\\sum_{t\\in T_i}|\\operatorname{Bag}_t|.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-174.1"
      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Multigraph cut** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a finite loopless undirected multigraph $G=(V,E)$ and a nonempty proper subset $S\subset V$, write $\delta_G(S)$ for the edges with exactly one endpoint in $S$.
- **Edge connectivity** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A graph is $k$-edge-connected if $\|\delta_G(S)\|\ge k$ for every nonempty proper $S$.
- **Thin spanning tree** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A spanning tree $T$ is $\alpha$-thin if $$\|\delta_T(S)\|\le\alpha\|\delta_G(S)\| \qquad(\varnothing\ne S\subsetneq V).$$
- **Tree packability** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): A graph is $r$-tree-packable if it contains $r$ pairwise edge-disjoint spanning trees.
- **Graph Laplacian** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Choose an orientation of each edge solely to define $b_e=\mathbf1_u-\mathbf1_v\in\mathbb R^V$, and put $$L_H=\sum_{e\in E(H)}b_eb_e^{\mathsf T}.$$
- **Resistance with respect to a positive definite matrix** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a real symmetric positive definite matrix $X$, define $$R_X(e)=b_e^{\mathsf T}X^{-1}b_e.$$
- **Hierarchy and local connectivity** ([source](#stmt-def-hierarchy)): Let $H=(V,E)$ be a finite loopless multigraph. A *hierarchy* on $V$ is a finite rooted tree of distinct nonempty vertex sets. Its root is $V$, its leaves are the singleton sets, and the children of every nonleaf form a partition of that node into at least two sets. For a nonroot node $A$, write $A^*$ for its parent and define $$P(A)=\delta_H(A),\qquad O(A)=E_H(A,A^*\setminus A).$$ All edge sets here retain multiplicities. Given an integer $h\ge2$, the hierarchy is *locally $h$-connected* if $H[A]$ is $h$-edge-connected for every node $A$. For singleton nodes this condition is vacuous.
- **Shortcut matrix** ([source](#eq:hierarchy-shortcut)): A *shortcut matrix* for $H$ means a symmetric positive definite matrix $D$ on $\mathbb R^V$ such that $$\label{eq:hierarchy-shortcut} \mathbf 1_S^{\mathsf T}D\mathbf 1_S\le \|\delta_H(S)\| \qquad(\varnothing\ne S\subsetneq V).$$
- **Compact response domain** ([source](#pm-h-11)): Let $$\label{eq:compact-domain} \mathcal K= \left\{X=X^{\mathsf T}: X\succeq L_H+n^{-1}I,\quad \mathbf1_S^{\mathsf T}X\mathbf1_S\le3\|\delta_H(S)\| \text{ for }\varnothing\ne S\subsetneq V\right\}.$$
- **Threshold graph and maximal packing partition** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For $s>0$, let $$F_s(X)=\bigl(V,\{e\in E(H):R_X(e)\le s\}\bigr), \qquad \mathcal P_s(X)=\mathcal P(F_s(X)),$$
- **Real stable polynomial** ([source](#pm-h-18)): A nonzero polynomial $p$ in several variables is *stable* if it does not vanish when all its arguments lie in $\mathbb H$.
- **Mixed characteristic polynomial** ([source](#eq:mss-mixed-polynomial)): For positive-semidefinite Hermitian $d$-by-$d$ matrices $A_1,\ldots,A_m$, define $$\mu[A_1,\ldots,A_m](x) =\left. \left(\prod_{i=1}^m(1-\partial_{z_i})\right) \det\left(xI_d+\sum_{i=1}^m z_iA_i\right) \right\|_{z_1=\cdots=z_m=0}. \label{eq:mss-mixed-polynomial}$$
- **Common interlacing** ([source](#pm-h-20)): A finite collection of such polynomials has a *common interlacing* if there are real numbers $c_1\leq\cdots\leq c_{d-1}$ such that $$\lambda_k(f)\leq c_k\leq\lambda_{k+1}(f) \quad(1\leq k<d)$$ for every polynomial in the collection.
- **Above-roots region and barrier functions** ([source](#pm-h-21)): For a real stable polynomial $p$, let $$\operatorname{Ab}_p =\{z\in\mathbb R^m:p(z+s)>0 \text{ for every }s\in[0,\infty)^m\}.$$
- **Balls, bags, assignment, compactness, and geometric mass** ([source](#stmt-hb-definitions)): Set $Q=[0,1]^d$ and $$B_Q(x,r)=\{y\in Q:\\|y-x\\|_1<r\}\qquad(r>0).$$ A *bag* is a nonempty collection of pairwise disjoint such balls, all of the same radius and with centers among the $X_v$. Its type is $(\delta)$ when that radius is $\delta$, and its type is $(\delta,\Delta)$ when, in addition, all distances between its centers are at most $\Delta$. Its size $\|\mathrm{Bag}\|$ is its number of balls. For $\beta>1$, a bag of type $(\delta,\Delta)$ is *$\beta$-compact* if $$\|\mathrm{Bag}\|\ge2,\qquad \beta\Delta\le\delta\|\mathrm{Bag}\|.$$ The midpoint of two centers belongs to $Q$, so disjoint radius-$\delta$ balls have centers at distance at least $2\delta$. Hence a compact bag has $\Delta\ge2\delta$. A bag of radius $\delta$ is *$\eta$-assigned to $t$* if $$\|\mathrm{Bag}_t\|\ge\eta\|O(t)\|,$$ and every ball has a center $X_u$ with $u\in V(t)$ and an edge $uv\in O(t)$ such that $d_{uv}<\delta$. A *family* of bags is nonempty, and all balls in all its bags are mutually disjoint. A compact family has type $(\delta,\Delta)$ when its bags have that common type. An assigned family has type $(\delta,T)$ when its bags are assigned to distinct nodes in $T$ and have radius $\delta$. For $0<\lambda<1$, a sequence of compact families of types $(\delta_i,\Delta_i)$ is *$\lambda$-geometric* if $\Delta_{i+1}<\lambda\delta_i$ for all successive families. A sequence of assigned families of types $(\delta_i,T_i)$ is $\lambda$-geometric if $\delta_{i+1}<\lambda\delta_i$ and the sets $T_i$ are pairwise disjoint. Its *mass* is $$\mathsf M=\sum_i\delta_i \sum_{\mathrm{Bag}\in\mathcal F_i}\|\mathrm{Bag}\|.$$
- **Bad nodes and homogeneous dominating subsets** ([source](#stmt-hb-bad)): For $0<\alpha\le1$, a nonroot node $t$ with $O(t)\ne\varnothing$ is *$\alpha$-bad* if $$\left(\frac{\sum_{e\in O(t)}a_e}{\|O(t)\|}\right)^2 \ge\frac{\alpha}{\|O(t)\|}\sum_{e\in O(t)}d_e.$$ Only nodes with a positive boundary sum need be retained. A set of edges is *$c$-homogeneous* if its $a_e^2$ are positive and any two of them have ratio less than $c$, and the same holds for its $d_e$. A subset $O'(t)\subseteq O(t)$ is *$\gamma$-dominating* if $$\left(\sum_{e\in O'(t)}a_e\right)^2 \ge\gamma\left(\sum_{e\in O(t)}a_e\right)^2.$$
- **Valid labeling** ([source](#stmt-ha-l4)): A collection $Z$ of labeled ordinary and hollow balls is valid if the following four conditions hold for every record $B$ with center $x$ and radii $a<b$. 1. The labels and conflict domain have the form just described; in particular $t(B)\in C(B)$. 2. For $s\in t_P(B)$ and $\{v,w\}\in P(s)$, $$\\|x-X_v\\|_1\ge b,\qquad \\|x-X_w\\|_1\ge b.$$ 3. There is $u\in L(B)$ with $\\|x-X_u\\|_1\le a$ and at least $k/4$ pairwise edge-disjoint paths starting at $u$ and ending at vertices of distance at least $b$ from $x$. The paths lie in $G[V(t(B))]$ if $B$ is nonavoiding, and in $G[V(t(B))\setminus V(t_d(B))]$ if it is avoiding. 4. Distinct records whose geometric balls intersect have disjoint conflict domains.

      "exact_definition": "<span id=\"ha:valid-labeling\" label=\"ha:valid-labeling\"></span> A collection $Z$ of labeled ordinary and hollow balls is valid if the following four conditions hold for every record $B$ with center $x$ and radii $a<b$.\n\n1.  <span id=\"ha:L1\" label=\"ha:L1\"></span> The labels and conflict domain have the form just described; in particular $t(B)\\in C(B)$.\n\n2.  <span id=\"ha:L2\" label=\"ha:L2\"></span> For $s\\in t_P(B)$ and $\\{v,w\\}\\in P(s)$, $$\\|x-X_v\\|_1\\ge b,\\qquad \\|x-X_w\\|_1\\ge b.$$\n\n3.  <span id=\"ha:L3\" label=\"ha:L3\"></span> There is $u\\in L(B)$ with $\\|x-X_u\\|_1\\le a$ and at least $k/4$ pairwise edge-disjoint paths starting at $u$ and ending at vertices of distance at least $b$ from $x$. The paths lie in $G[V(t(B))]$ if $B$ is nonavoiding, and in $G[V(t(B))\\setminus V(t_d(B))]$ if it is avoiding.\n\n4.  Distinct records whose geometric balls intersect have disjoint conflict domains.",
      "interpretation": "Record labels specify routing domains, excluded predecessor boundaries, crossing-path witnesses, and separation of conflict domains for intersecting geometric records.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Valid labeling",
      "scope": "Assigned-bag charging."
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-174.1",
  "schema": "pm.objects.v2"
}
```

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-universal-thinness | Spanning-tree cut size | ≤ | $\frac Ck\,\|\delta_G(S)\|$ | The constant is universal; the same tree satisfies every cut bound. |
| q-hierarchy-average-resistance | Marked parent-boundary average resistance | ≤ | $q^{-31/40}$ | The absolute cutoff is independent of the graph, depth, and marked collection. |
| q-extraction-relative-loss | Extraction loss parameter | = | $r^{-1/16}$ | The packing parameter is above an absolute threshold. |
| q-extracted-packing-count | Extracted packing target | = | $\lfloor(1-u)r\rfloor$ | The loss parameter is the one defined for extraction. |
| q-extraction-cut-upper-bound | Extraction matrix cut quadratic form | ≤ | $3\|\delta_H(S)\|$ | Only nonempty proper cuts are constrained. |
| q-extraction-edge-threshold | Resistance threshold retaining the extracted packing | = | $4r^{-1/2}$ | Edges below the threshold contain the extracted packing. |
| q-response-constant | Admissible simultaneous-response constant | = | $40$ | The constant is absolute, in the extraction section's large-packing regime. |
| q-isotropic-vector-norm | Squared norm of an auxiliary edge vector | ≤ | $\frac{1+\rho}{q}$ | The representation is indexed by individual edges. |
| q-projected-trace-packing | Guaranteed edge-disjoint spanning-tree count | ≥ | $\lfloor cq/(1+\rho)\rfloor$ | The spectral lower bound and auxiliary norm bound hold for the same subset. |
| q-two-sided-split-error | Two-sided covariance-splitting error | = | $2\sqrt\epsilon+\epsilon$ | The same selected subset satisfies the upper and lower matrix bounds. |
| q-sparsified-packing-count | Guaranteed retained packing count | = | $\left\lfloor\frac{(1-4u)r}{2}\right\rfloor$ | The loss and splitting parameters are those of the sparsification proposition. |
| q-sparsification-cut-factor | Sparsified cut contraction factor | = | $\frac12+3\tau$ | The bound holds simultaneously on every proper nonempty cut. |
| q-sparsification-relative-loss | Cut contraction divided by packing contraction | ≤ | $1+15u$ | The cutoff is chosen to ensure the additional ratio inequalities. |
| q-iteration-loss-constant | Accumulated iteration loss | = | $\exp\left( \frac{15r_*^{-1/16}}{1-2^{-1/16}} \right)$ | The stopping threshold is at least the sparsification cutoff. |
| q-mss-operator-norm | Operator norm in a successful independent-vector outcome | ≤ | $(1+\sqrt\epsilon)^2$ | The covariance sum is exactly isotropic and every expected squared norm satisfies the bound. |
| q-shortcut-dual-comparison | Binary-embedding supremum | ≤ | $2p_*$ | This compares optimization values; it is not a pointwise identity for an arbitrary embedding. |
| q-spectral-packing-loss | Power-form spectral-packing loss constant | = | $\frac{1024}{\varepsilon^2}$ | The same constant controls the ball count and squared-radius mass. |
| q-homogeneous-dominance | Retained squared-boundary-sum fraction | = | $\frac1{4J^4}$ | The bucketing parameter is defined in the same statement. |
| q-compact-scale-separation | Compact-bag geometric scale ratio | ≤ | $1/12$ | This is a hypothesis of the compact charging theorem. |
| q-assigned-constant-choice | Admissible assigned-bag separation constant | = | $10^4$ | The companion constant and geometric-ratio restriction must also hold. |
| q-hierarchy-energy-mass | Common energy-to-mass charging coefficient | = | $\frac1{320000}$ | The connectivity parameter exceeds the absolute cutoff, and either charging construction applies. |
| q-total-dual-decay | Marked-node numerator divided by embedding energy | = | $o(q^{-31/40})$ | The connectivity parameter grows beyond an absolute cutoff under the stated parameter choice. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-resistance-order** [claim](#stmt-lem-resistance-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-resistance-order): Congruence reduces the scalar bound to a rank-one eigenvalue bound; inversion reverses positive definite order. -> Resistance and semidefinite comparisons.
- **step-incidence-rank** [claim](#stmt-lem-incidence-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-incidence-rank): Identify the orthogonal complement of incidence columns as vectors constant on connected components. -> Incidence rank and the spanning-tree basis property.
- **step-packing-criterion** [claim](#stmt-thm-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-packing): Maximize the union of disjoint forests and use cycle exchanges to expose a violating partition unless all forests span. -> The spanning-tree packing criterion.
- **step-packing-parts** [claim](#stmt-lem-packing-parts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-packing-parts): Merge overlapping packed sets by contraction, then apply the partition criterion in a minimal dense quotient. -> Maximal packing partitions and the strict quotient crossing bound.
- **step-polynomial-limits** [claim](#stmt-lem-mss-polynomial-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mss-polynomial-limits): Pass to convergent root subsequences, allowing roots to escape to infinity, and use uniqueness of ordered roots. -> Preservation of zero exclusion and continuity of ordered roots.
- **step-stability-operations** [claim](#stmt-lem-mss-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mss-stability): Use a positive imaginary matrix part, logarithmic derivatives of slices, and limits of real specializations. -> The stability operations used by mixed characteristic polynomials.
- **step-expectation-identity** [claim](#stmt-lem-mss-expectation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mss-expectation): Apply stability-preserving differential operators and conditionally iterate the rank-one determinant identity. -> A real-rooted monic expected characteristic polynomial.
- **step-common-interlacing** [claim](#stmt-lem-mss-common-interlacing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mss-common-interlacing): Track root counts through an affine polynomial segment and rule out multiple crossings using polynomial limits. -> Common interlacing and a largest-root selection rule.
- **step-independent-selection** [claim](#stmt-lem-mss-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mss-selection): Condition on successive support choices and choose a child whose largest root does not increase. -> A positive-probability complete outcome with controlled largest eigenvalue.
- **step-positive-partial-fractions** [claim](#stmt-lem-mss-partial-fractions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mss-partial-fractions): Classify the rational barrier by its upper-half-plane imaginary part, negative poles, and nonnegativity on the positive ray. -> Positive partial fractions and barrier monotonicity and convexity.
- **step-barrier-shift** [claim](#stmt-lem-mss-barrier-shift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mss-barrier-shift): Combine logarithmic differentiation with monotonicity and the tangent inequality supplied by convexity. -> An above-roots point after differentiation and a controlled coordinate shift.
- **step-mixed-root-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-mss-root-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mss-root-bound): Start on the diagonal above the roots and apply the barrier shift successively in every coordinate. -> The mixed characteristic polynomial largest-root bound.
- **step-mss-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-mss) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mss-root-bound-2): Bound the mixed polynomial's largest root and select an independent-support outcome realizing that bound. -> The isotropic independent-vector norm theorem.
- **step-dual-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lem-hierarchy-dual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hierarchy-dual): Use finite-dimensional separation, evaluate the matrix infimum, approximate weighted cuts by repeated binary coordinates, and exploit boundary overlap. -> Comparison of the shortcut infimum with the binary-embedding supremum.
- **step-spectral-packing** [claim](#stmt-hb-spectral-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hb-spectral-packing): Select a singular-value tail, fix its radius, and contradict low-rank approximation if a maximal separated set is too small. -> Disjoint Euclidean balls with count and squared-radius mass bounds.
- **step-homogeneous-truncation** [claim](#stmt-hb-bucketing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hb-bucketing): Discard small signal and large energy tails, then retain a dyadic pair of buckets with enough boundary sum. -> A homogeneous dominating boundary subset.
- **step-homogeneous-bags** [claim](#stmt-hb-homogeneous-bags) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hb-homogeneous-bags): Apply spectral packing to contracted images, normalize mass, and distinguish clustered compact bags from numerous assigned centers. -> A compact or assigned family with controlled mass and radius range.
- **step-geometric-bags** [claim](#stmt-prop-hierarchy-bags) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hierarchy-bags): Group homogeneous data by dyadic scales, retain a dominant energy-scale group, and select a separated residue class of one bag type. -> A geometric sequence capturing the bad-node numerator.
- **step-disjoint-shell-length** [claim](#stmt-hc-length) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hc-length): Integrate radial cut boundaries and bound total variation along fixed edge segments using geometric disjointness. -> A length budget for total radial width.
- **step-compact-charging** [claim](#stmt-prop-hierarchy-compact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hc-length-2): Insert bags while splitting old shells, maintain capacity and witness invariants, and pass from finite initial sequences to a nonnegative supremum. -> Graph length bounds compact-bag mass.
- **step-edge-paths** [claim](#stmt-ha-edge-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ha-edge-paths): Augment an integral antisymmetric flow and extract positive-flow paths after reaching the required value. -> Pairwise edge-disjoint connecting paths.
- **step-pruning** [claim](#stmt-ha-pruning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ha-pruning): Inject noninsertable balls at a scale into boundary endpoints and sum the geometrically decreasing radii. -> A predecessor-by-predecessor pruning mass bound.
- **step-labeled-energy** [claim](#stmt-ha-labeled-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ha-labeled-energy): Stop witness paths at radial exits, retain shell portions, and use conflict-domain separation to avoid charging a graph edge portion twice. -> Graph length bounds total valid labeled width.
- **step-input-validity** [claim](#stmt-ha-input-valid) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ha-input-valid): Use insertability for predecessor exclusion and local connectivity for paths to another bag center. -> Individual validity of each insertable input record.
- **step-ancestor-relation** [claim](#stmt-ha-ancestor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ha-ancestor): Compare roots of intersecting rooted domains, exclude earlier descendant owners through predecessor labels, and use the depth order. -> The existing owner is an ancestor of the current bag node.
- **step-unique-interior** [claim](#stmt-ha-unique-interior) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ha-unique-interior): An intersecting pair of current records cannot both contain the current node in their conflict domains. -> Uniqueness of a candidate's interior record.
- **step-heredity** [claim](#stmt-ha-heredity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ha-heredity): Restrict supports and conflict domains, retaining the old witnesses and paths across a smaller radial interval. -> Inherited pairwise separation and individual validity.
- **step-border-monotonicity** [claim](#stmt-ha-border-monotone) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ha-border-monotone): Check that original inserted balls are too narrow to create interiors and every other new support is a concentric restriction. -> Border candidates remain border throughout the phase.
- **step-interior-construction** [claim](#stmt-ha-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ha-construction): Carve avoiding shells, insert dense radial groups, or split sparse groups into complementary routing domains while transferring old allocations. -> Valid interior processing with preserved old credit and new credit for treated balls.
- **step-shrink-deficit** [claim](#stmt-ha-shrink-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ha-shrink-cost): Compare the gain from decreasing potential thresholds against the deficit from concentric restrictions, record by record. -> A nonnegative shrink deficit payable from border-ball credit.
- **step-insertable-arrangement** [claim](#stmt-ha-insertable-arrangement) / [proof](#ha:postprocess): Complete every interior loop, supply border credit from excess potential or shrink-and-insert, and induct over the finite sequence of phases. -> A finite valid arrangement whose width captures retained bag mass.
- **step-assigned-charging** [claim](#stmt-prop-hierarchy-assigned) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ha-insertable-arrangement): Prune noninsertable balls, retain sufficiently populated bags, build an insertable arrangement, and apply labeled energy charging. -> Graph length bounds original assigned-bag mass.
- **step-hierarchy-power** [claim](#stmt-thm-hierarchy-power) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ha-shrink-cost-2): Split marked nodes by badness, capture the bad numerator in geometric bags, charge either bag type, and use strict slack in the dual reduction. -> A shortcut with polynomially small marked-boundary average resistance.
- **step-simultaneous-response** [claim](#stmt-lem-response) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-response): Choose partition representatives by dyadic crossing counts, form a locally connected hierarchy, and sum disappearing-edge bad counts through a geometric tail. -> A response working for all threshold crossing sets at once.
- **step-sperner** [claim](#stmt-lemma-sperner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-sperner): Count incidences with specially labeled facets modulo parity and induct on the boundary face dimension. -> Odd parity of fully labeled simplices.
- **step-fixed-point** [claim](#stmt-thm-fixedpoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-sperner-2): Use fully labeled simplices in subdivisions of shrinking mesh, take a compactness limit, and extend by continuous nearest-point projection. -> A fixed point for a continuous self-map of the compact convex domain.
- **step-extraction** [claim](#stmt-thm-extraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-response-2): Interpolate finitely many responses, take fixed points and a subsequential limit, stabilize an avoided threshold, and contradict the quotient crossing bound. -> A shortcut whose low-resistance edges retain the extracted packing.
- **step-isotropic-representation** [claim](#stmt-lem-auxiliary-representation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-auxiliary-representation): Regularize a bounded-below log-determinant objective, choose a point with small gradient, and normalize using strictly positive weights. -> An isotropic edge-vector representation preserving every subset rank.
- **step-projected-trace** [claim](#stmt-lem-projected-trace-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-projected-trace-packing): Project orthogonally away from within-part vectors, take traces, and invoke the packing partition criterion. -> A spanning-tree packing certified by the selected vector covariance.
- **step-two-sided-split** [claim](#stmt-lemma-two-system-split) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lemma-two-system-split): Randomly place each vector into a coordinate block, pad the covariance to the identity, apply the independent-vector theorem, and subtract deterministic padding. -> A single subset with two-sided covariance control.
- **step-sparsification** [claim](#stmt-prop-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-step): Stack low-resistance incidence vectors with auxiliary packing vectors, split the same system, test one block on cuts, and project the other block to certify packing. -> An unweighted spanning subgraph with comparable cut and packing contraction.
- **step-thin-tree-iteration** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-step-2): Iterate packing-preserving sparsification until the absolute stopping threshold, bound the product of relative losses, and obtain the initial packing from edge connectivity. -> A spanning tree with a universal inverse-connectivity all-cut bound.

          "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a nonempty set of oriented edges of a finite multigraph with edge set $E$, and put $m=|F|$. Suppose that $a>0$ and $$\\label{hb:spectral-assumption}\n \\Upsilon:=\\left(\\frac1m\\sum_{e\\in F}Y_{e,e}\\right)^2>0,\n \\qquad\n \\Upsilon\\ge\\frac a m\\sum_{e\\in F}\\|Y_e\\|_2^2.$$ Then $a\\le1$, and there are $b\\ge2$ pairwise disjoint Euclidean balls of one positive squared radius $r$, centered at images of endpoints of edges in $F$, such that $$\\label{hb:spectral-logarithmic}\n b\\ge\\frac{am}{16},\n \\qquad\n br\\ge\\frac{9m\\Upsilon}\n {512\\bigl(5+\\log_2(1/a)\\bigr)^2}.$$ Consequently, for every $0<\\varepsilon<1/3$, the choice $$\\label{hb:spectral-constant}\n C_1(\\varepsilon)=\\frac{1024}{\\varepsilon^2}$$ gives $$\\label{hb:spectral-conclusion}\n b\\ge\\frac{a|F|}{C_1(\\varepsilon)},\n \\qquad\n br\\ge\\frac{a^\\varepsilon\\Upsilon|F|}{C_1(\\varepsilon)}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-174.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-spectral-packing",
      "inputs": [
        "Endpoint images with positive diagonal signal relative to edge energy."
      ],
      "method": "Select a singular-value tail, fix its radius, and contradict low-rank approximation if a maximal separated set is too small.",
      "output": "Disjoint Euclidean balls with count and squared-radius mass bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-hb-spectral-packing"
    },

Write $$s=\\frac{\\alpha\\gamma}{32},\\qquad\n c_1=\\min_{e\\in F}a_e^2,\n \\qquad\n S'=\\sum_{t\\in T}\\frac1{|O(t)|}\n                     \\left(\\sum_{e\\in O'(t)}a_e\\right)^2.$$ For $\\beta>1$ and $0<\\epsilon<1/3$, put $C=C_1(\\epsilon)=1024\\epsilon^{-2}$. If $$\\label{hb:smallness}\n s^\\epsilon\\le\\frac1{48\\beta C},$$ there is either a family of $\\beta$-compact bags or a family of $s^{1+2\\epsilon}$-assigned bags, assigned to a subset of $T$, whose mass is at least $$\\label{hb:one-mass}\n \\frac{s^\\epsilon}{192\\beta C}\\,S'.$$ Its radius, and also its diameter parameter in the compact case, belong to $[sc_1,12c_1/s]$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-hierarchy-bags",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a nonempty finite set of $\\alpha$-bad nodes with positive boundary sums, where $0<\\alpha\\le1$. Define $$\\begin{aligned}\n J&=1+\\lceil\\log_2(256/\\alpha^2)\\rceil,\n &C_2(\\alpha)&=128J^4,\n &s&=\\alpha/C_2(\\alpha),\\\\\n L&=2+\\lceil\\log_2(1/s)\\rceil,\n &H&=1+\\lceil\\log_2(24/(\\lambda s^2))\\rceil,\n &C_1(\\epsilon)&=1024\\epsilon^{-2}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Suppose $\\beta>1$, $0<\\epsilon<1/3$, $0<\\lambda<1$, and $s^\\epsilon\\le(48\\beta C_1(\\epsilon))^{-1}$. There is a $\\lambda$-geometric sequence of families, consisting either entirely of $\\beta$-compact bags or entirely of $s^{1+2\\epsilon}$-assigned bags assigned to distinct nodes of $T$, whose mass satisfies $$\\label{hb:extraction-bound}\n \\mathsf M\\ge\n \\frac{s^\\epsilon}{384\\beta C_1(\\epsilon)C_2(\\alpha)LH}\n \\sum_{t\\in T}\\frac1{|O(t)|}\n                  \\left(\\sum_{e\\in O(t)}a_e\\right)^2.$$ In particular, all losses are functions of $\\alpha,\\epsilon,\\beta$ and $\\lambda$, independent of the number of vertices and hierarchy depth.",

          "anchor": "stmt-hc-length",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G=(V,E)$ be a finite $k$-edge-connected multigraph, where $k>0$, and let $X_v\\in Q=[0,1]^h$ for $v\\in V$. Let $\\mathcal Z$ be a finite collection of pairwise disjoint ordinary or hollowed balls in $Q$. For each $H\\in\\mathcal Z$, write $x_H,a_H,b_H$ for its center and numerical inner and outer radii. Suppose there are vertices $u_H,v_H\\in V$ such that $$\\label{hc:witness}\n \\|X_{u_H}-x_H\\|_1\\le a_H,\n \\qquad \\|X_{v_H}-x_H\\|_1\\ge b_H.$$ Then $$\\label{hc:length-bound}\n k\\sum_{H\\in\\mathcal Z}(b_H-a_H)\n \\le \\sum_{\\{u,v\\}\\in E}\\|X_u-X_v\\|_1.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-174.1"
        }

      "id": "step-disjoint-shell-length",

Suppose $(\\mathcal F_i)$ is a finite or countable $\\lambda$-geometric sequence of nonempty families of $\\beta$-compact bags, of respective types $(\\delta_i,\\Delta_i)$, where $0<\\lambda\\le 1/12$ and $\\beta\\ge36$. Thus all balls in $\\mathcal F_i$ are mutually disjoint ordinary balls $B_Q(X_v,\\delta_i)$, every bag $D\\in\\mathcal F_i$ has at least two balls and center diameter at most $\\Delta_i$, and $$\\beta\\Delta_i\\le |D|\\delta_i,\n \\qquad\n \\Delta_{i+1}\\le\\lambda\\delta_i.$$ Then $$\\label{hc:conclusion}\n \\frac{k}{4}\\sum_i\\delta_i\\sum_{D\\in\\mathcal F_i}|D|\n \\le \\sum_{e\\in E}\\|X_e\\|_1,\n \\qquad X_{\\{u,v\\}}=X_u-X_v.$$",

Let $\\mathcal F_i$ be a $\\lambda$-geometric sequence of families of $12C_3/k$-assigned bags of type $(\\delta_i,T_i)$, where the $T_i\\subseteq T$ are pairwise disjoint.       "output": "A response working for all threshold crossing sets at once.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-response"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lemma-sperner",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lemma-sperner",
          "exact_quote": "Label every vertex $v$ of a finite triangulation of $\\Delta_d$ by an integer $\\ell(v)\\in\\{0,\\ldots,d\\}$, with $v_{\\ell(v)}>0$. The number of $d$-simplices whose vertex labels are $0,\\ldots,d$, each occurring once, is odd.",
          "source_id": "PM-174.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-sperner",
      "inputs": [
        "A finite simplex triangulation with face-compatible vertex labels."
      ],
      "method": "Count incidences with specially labeled facets modulo parity and induct on the boundary face dimension.",
      "output": "Odd parity of fully labeled simplices.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lemma-sperner"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-fixedpoint",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-sperner"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-fixedpoint",
          "exact_quote": "Let $K$ be a nonempty compact convex subset of a finite-dimensional real vector space. Every continuous map $F:K\\to K$ has a fixed point.",
          "source_id": "PM-174.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-fixed-point",
      "inputs": [
        "Sperner parity.",
        "Compactness, convexity, and continuity."
      ],
      "method": "Use fully labeled simplices in subdivisions of shrinking mesh, take a compactness limit, and extend by continuous nearest-point projection.",
      "output": "A fixed point for a continuous self-map of the compact convex domain.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lemma-sperner-2"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-extraction",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-simultaneous-response",
        "step-fixed-point",
        "step-packing-parts",
        "step-resistance-order"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-extraction",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large integers $r$, every $r$-tree-packable graph $H$ admits $X\\succ0$ such that $$\\label{eq:shortcut-X}\n L_H\\preceq X,\\qquad\n \\mathbf1_S^{\\mathsf T}X\\mathbf1_S\\le3|\\delta_H(S)|\n \\quad(\\varnothing\\ne S\\subsetneq V),$$ and the edges with $R_X(e)\\le4r^{-1/2}$ contain $q$ edge-disjoint spanning trees. The lower threshold on $r$ is independent of $H$ and $n$.",
          "source_id": "PM-174.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-extraction",
      "inputs": [
        "The simultaneous response.",
        "The compact convex response domain.",
        "The fixed-point theorem.",
        "Maximal packing partitions and resistance order."
      ],
      "method": "Interpolate finitely many responses, take fixed points and a subsequential limit, stabilize an avoided threshold, and contradict the quotient crossing bound.",
      "output": "A shortcut whose low-resistance edges retain the extracted packing.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-response-2"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-auxiliary-representation",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-incidence-rank"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-auxiliary-representation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be the union of $q\\geq1$ edge-disjoint spanning trees on an $n$-vertex set, where $n\\geq2$. For every $\\rho>0$ there are vectors $z_e\\in\\mathbb R^{n-1}$, indexed by the individual edges of $B$, such that $$\\sum_{e\\in B}z_ez_e^{\\mathsf T}=I_{n-1},\n \\qquad \\|z_e\\|^2\\leq\\frac{1+\\rho}{q}.\n \\label{eq:auxiliary-isotropy}$$ Moreover, there are an invertible linear map $M:\\mathbf1^\\perp\\to\n\\mathbb R^{n-1}$ and positive numbers $s_e$ such that $z_e=s_eMb_e$. Consequently the incidence vectors and the $z_e$ have the same rank on every subset of edges.",
          "source_id": "PM-174.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-isotropic-representation",
      "inputs": [
        "A union of edge-disjoint spanning trees.",
        "Incidence rank."
      ],
      "method": "Regularize a bounded-below log-determinant objective, choose a point with small gradient, and normalize using strictly positive weights.",
      "output": "An isotropic edge-vector representation preserving every subset rank.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-auxiliary-representation"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-projected-trace-packing",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-isotropic-representation",
        "step-packing-criterion"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-projected-trace-packing",
          "exact_quote": "With the vectors in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:auxiliary-representation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:auxiliary-representation\">11</a>, suppose $A\\subseteq E(B)$ and $c>0$ satisfy $$\\sum_{e\\in A}z_ez_e^{\\mathsf T}\\succeq cI_{n-1}.$$ Then $(V,A)$ contains at least $\\lfloor cq/(1+\\rho)\\rfloor$ edge-disjoint spanning trees.",
          "source_id": "PM-174.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-projected-trace",
      "inputs": [
        "The rank-preserving isotropic representation.",
        "A spectral lower bound on an edge subset."
      ],
      "method": "Project orthogonally away from within-part vectors, take traces, and invoke the packing partition criterion.",
      "output": "A spanning-tree packing certified by the selected vector covariance.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-projected-trace-packing"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lemma-two-system-split",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-mss-theorem"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lemma-two-system-split",
          "exact_quote": "Let $w_e\\in\\mathbb R^D$ be finitely many vectors, let $\\Sigma=\\sum_e w_ew_e^{\\mathsf T}\\preceq2I_D$, and suppose that $\\|w_e\\|^2\\leq\\epsilon$ for every $e$, where $\\epsilon>0$. There is a subset $A$ of the indices for which $$\\frac12\\Sigma-\\tau I_D\n \\preceq\\sum_{e\\in A}w_ew_e^{\\mathsf T}\n \\preceq\\frac12\\Sigma+\\tau I_D,\n \\qquad \\tau=2\\sqrt\\epsilon+\\epsilon.\n \\label{eq:two-sided-split}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-174.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-two-sided-split",
      "inputs": [
        "A bounded covariance and bounded vector norms.",
        "The isotropic independent-vector theorem."
      ],
      "method": "Randomly place each vector into a coordinate block, pad the covariance to the identity, apply the independent-vector theorem, and subtract deterministic padding.",
      "output": "A single subset with two-sided covariance control.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lemma-two-system-split"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-step",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-extraction",
        "step-isotropic-representation",
        "step-projected-trace",
        "step-two-sided-split"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-step",
          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute integer $r_{\\mathrm{step}}$ such that the following holds for every integer $r\\geq r_{\\mathrm{step}}$. If $H$ has at least two vertices and contains $r$ edge-disjoint spanning trees, set $$u=r^{-1/16},\\qquad q=\\lfloor(1-u)r\\rfloor,\n \\qquad \\epsilon=6r^{-1/2},\\qquad\n \\tau=2\\sqrt\\epsilon+\\epsilon.$$ Then $H$ has a spanning subgraph $H'$ containing at least $$r'=\\left\\lfloor\\frac{(1-4u)r}{2}\\right\\rfloor\n \\label{eq:step-packing-count}$$ edge-disjoint spanning trees, such that every nonempty proper $S\\subset V(H)$ satisfies $$|\\delta_{H'}(S)|\\leq\n \\left(\\frac12+3\\tau\\right)|\\delta_H(S)|.\n \\label{eq:step-cut-bound}$$ The cutoff may be chosen so that, in addition, $$\\frac5{16}\\leq\\frac{r'}r\\leq\\frac12,\n \\qquad\n \\frac{\\frac12+3\\tau}{r'/r}\\leq1+15u.\n \\label{eq:step-ratio-bound}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-174.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-sparsification",
      "inputs": [
        "Low-resistance extraction.",
        "Rank-preserving isotropy.",
        "Projected-trace packing.",
        "Two-sided splitting."
      ],
      "method": "Stack low-resistance incidence vectors with auxiliary packing vectors, split the same system, test one block on cuts, and project the other block to certify packing.",
      "output": "An unweighted spanning subgraph with comparable cut and packing contraction.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-step"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-sparsification",
        "step-packing-criterion"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "There is a universal constant $C>0$ such that, for every integer $k\\ge1$, every finite $k$-edge-connected undirected multigraph without loops and with at least two vertices has a spanning tree $T$ satisfying $$|\\delta_T(S)|\\le\\frac Ck\\,|\\delta_G(S)|\n \\qquad\\text{for every }\\varnothing\\ne S\\subsetneq V.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-174.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-thin-tree-iteration",
      "inputs": [
        "The sparsification step.",
        "The partition packing criterion.",
        "The small-connectivity cases."
      ],
      "method": "Iterate packing-preserving sparsification until the absolute stopping threshold, bound the product of relative losses, and obtain the initial packing from edge connectivity.",
      "output": "A spanning tree with a universal inverse-connectivity all-cut bound.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-step-2"
    }
  ]
}
```
